28th December 1937 – 9th October 20243rd March 1839 – 19th May 1904Padma Vibhushan
Mr. Ratan N TataJamsetji Nusserwanji Tata
It is with a profound sense of loss that we bid farewell to Mr. Ratan Naval Tata, 
a truly uncommon leader whose immeasurable contributions have shaped not only the  
Tata Group but also the very fabric of our nation.
For the Tata Group, Mr. Tata was more than a chairperson. He inspired by example.  
With an unwavering commitment to excellence, integrity and innovation, the 
Tata Group under his stewardship expanded its global footprint while always remaining 
true to its moral compass.
Mr. Tata’s dedication to philanthropy and the development of society has touched the lives 
of millions. From education to healthcare, his initiatives have left a deep‑rooted mark that 
will benefit generations to come. Reinforcing all of this mark was Mr. Tata’s genuine humility  
in every individual interaction. 
His legacy will continue to inspire us as we strive to uphold the principles 
he so passionately championed.
=== page 4 ===
About
the Report
Reporting Approach
This Report provides our stakeholders with a 
holistic view of Trent Limited's (the Company 
or Trent) ability to create value, utilising both 
financial and non ‑financial resources. The 
Report offers visibility to our performance, 
business strategy, operating environment, 
material issues and mitigation approach, risks 
and opportunities, governance structure, and our 
approach to long‑term sustainability.
Framework, Guidelines,  
and Standards
The Report is guided by the principles of 
Integrated Reporting laid out by the International 
Integrated Reporting Council (IIRC).  
The statutory reports, including the Board's 
Report, Management Discussion & Analysis 
(MD&A), Corporate Governance Report, 
and Business Responsibility & Sustainability 
Report (BRSR Report), and Financial Statements, 
are in line with the Indian Accounting Standards, 
the Companies Act, 2013, Securities Exchange 
Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015, and the 
prescribed Secretarial Standards issued by the 
Institute of Company Secretaries of India.
Scope and Reporting Boundary
The Report covers Standalone Financials 
pertaining to Trent as well as Consolidated 
Financials for the subsidiaries, associates, and 
group companies for the period 1st April 2024 
to 31st March 2025.
The Financial Statements (Standalone and 
Consolidated) have been audited by Deloitte 
Haskins & Sells, LLP, Chartered Accountants.
Non‑financial information has been reported 
primarily on a standalone basis for Trent. Given 
the materiality and future growth agenda, we 
have also included select coverage on our food 
business under the Star banner. BDO India LLP has provided reasonable 
assurance of the non ‑financial information in 
the BRSR Report (BRSR Core indicators) for 
the period in reference. The assurance is in 
accordance with the International Standard on 
Assurance Engagements (ISAE) 3000 (Revised), 
Assurance Engagements Other than Audits 
or Reviews of Historical Financial Information 
and ISAE 3410, Assurance Engagements on 
Greenhouse Gas Statement issued by the 
International Auditing and Standards Board.
Leadership Accountability
Our Senior Management and the Managing 
Director have reviewed the contents of the Report 
while the Board Members have provided requisite 
strategic and governance oversight.
Forward-looking Statements
The Report includes certain statements regarding 
our business operations that may constitute 
forward ‑looking statements, including those 
regarding the financial position, business 
strategy, management plans, and objectives 
for future operations. We cannot guarantee that 
these forward ‑looking statements will be realised, 
although we believe we have been prudent in 
our assumptions. The achievements of the same 
are subject to risks, uncertainties, and inaccurate 
assumptions. We undertake no obligation to 
publicly update any forward ‑looking statements, 
whether as a result of new information, future 
events, or otherwise.
Feedback
We welcome any questions or feedback you may 
have about our performance or the Report.  
You may write to us at:  
investor.relations@trent ‑tata.com

space. All our brands share a common purpose: we put our customers first. 
Through a variety of product categories and style offerings, we aim to serve 
our consumers in a responsible manner. 
Our strategic choices differentiate us: obsession with delivering fresh and 
desirable products, credible quality, and offering compelling customer value 
propositions are defining attributes of our brand portfolio.

Message from
The Chairman
Dear Shareholders,
As I reflect on FY25, I am pleased to share that we continue to register progress across our brands, 
concepts, categories, and channels. 
Our own ‑branded apparel and lifestyle offerings now touch even more lives across the country.  
The Indian consumer has evolved rapidly in recent years and is seeking aspirational product 
propositions, attractive pricing, and importantly, ready accessibility. We are growing the desirability 
of our offerings and building the density of our presence across key micro ‑markets to serve our 
customers better.
As we expand our footprint in India and take our first steps into international markets, we remain 
committed to balancing profitability with sustainable development and meaningful social impact.  
These principles will continue to shape our business and the stakeholder partnerships, as we chart 
our future growth path.
Two years ago, I had envisioned that Trent would one day be ten times bigger. Since then, the revenue run 
rate has doubled. The head room for growth remains enormous, and I am confident that we will reach this 
milestone in the not too distant future.
NOEL N. TATA
ChairmanFY25

=== page 10 ===
Dear Shareholders,
I am pleased to present Trent’s Integrated Annual Report for FY25, which outlines our performance 
across both financial and other stakeholder dimensions.
FY25 was again a relatively challenging year for retail in India. Consumers faced multiple headwinds, 
including elevated inflation levels that impacted discretionary spends. Nevertheless, our brands 
continued to gain encouraging traction, including in several Tier 2 and Tier 3 geographies that we 
entered. Both Westside and Zudio now operate at scale and are witnessing strong volume ‑led growth 
across diverse categories. Operating from over 1,000 large ‑format fashion stores, we have engaged 
with more than 100 million customers.
Beyond our financial performance, we remain committed to embedding sustainability into our growth 
strategy, with imperatives ranging from responsible design and sourcing to investments in technology, 
energy ‑efficient stores, and process transformation. Our people agenda focuses on creating an 
inclusive workplace, ensuring health and safety, and strengthening community engagement. 
We are passionately committed to enhancing the desirability of our offerings, the accessibility of our 
presence, and the resilience of our platform. We are investing ahead of the curve – both in talent and 
technology. Differentiated by our choices and disciplines, and operating in a substantially growing 
market, we believe there is much reason to be excited about the puzzles to be solved and the 
opportunities to be seized.
P. VENKATESALU
Managing Director

About
Trent 
Trent is one of the leading players in the branded lifestyle space in India. We own 
and operate multiple brands that bring quality, affordability, and enhanced customer 
experience to a diverse consumer audience.
Our key concepts in the fashion space include Westside, Zudio and Samoh, and in the 
food and grocery space, we operate under the Star banner.
Touch lives across choice markets with a portfolio of fashion and lifestyle brandsVision
Build brands that are fresh, responsible and bring joy to allMission  
Integrity, Unity, Responsibility, Pioneering and ExcellenceValues

Westside, one of Trent’s leading 
lifestyle concepts, offers own branded 
fashion apparel, beauty, footwear, 
and accessories for women, men 
and children, along with a wide 
range of home furnishings and decor. 
Westside has an active presence in all 
aspects of the value chain, including 
design, production, supply chain, 
own distribution, and deep customer 
connect. It offers a differentiated 
portfolio of exclusively owned brands 
that are in sync with latest fashion 
trends, appealing to a wide spectrum 
of style conscious consumers across

=== page 52 ===
Our philosophy on Corporate Governance is to uphold the highest standards of ethics, 
fairness, and transparency in all our dealings and to ensure efficient business conduct 
with a robust governance structure to achieve the goal of maximising value for  
all stakeholders. Strong leadership and effective corporate governance practices are 
guided by the Tata culture and ethos.
Our Values
We will be fair, honest, transparent and ethical in our conduct; everything we do must 
stand the test of public scrutiny.
Integrity
We will be bold and agile, courageously taking on challenges, using deep customer 
insight to develop innovative solutions.
Pioneering
We will invest in our people and partners, enable continuous learning, and build caring 
and collaborative relationships based on trust and mutual respect.
Unity
We will integrate environmental and social principles in our businesses, ensuring that 
what comes from the people goes back to the people many times over.
Responsibility
We will be passionate about achieving the highest standards of quality, always 
promoting meritocracy.

MALE FEMALEBoard’s Responsibility
We have an experienced, diverse, and 
well‑informed Board. The Board along with 
its Committees undertakes its fiduciary 
duties keeping in mind the interests of all its 
stakeholders. The  Board periodically reviews the 
strategic, operational, financial, and governance 
matters of the Company and its key subsidiaries. 
The Chairperson of the respective Committees 
provides an update to the Board at each meeting 
about the discussion and decisions taken at the 
Committee meetings.
Board Composition
The Board comprises eight Directors, including  
five Independent Directors that constitutes 
62.5% of the Board, two Non ‑Executive 
Non‑Independent Directors (including the 
Chairman), and a Managing Director.
The Board Members possess broad ‑based 
business knowledge, strong relationships, 
prominence, and excellent reputation in their 
respective fields. They bring value additions, 
diverse range of expertise, and perspectives 
that enable them to discharge their oversight 
role effectively.
GenderBoard Committees 
There are seven Board Committees that are 
integral to effective corporate governance. 
They enable the Board to manage complex 
responsibilities through focused oversight 
and specialised expertise in the key areas 
such as audit, risk management, nomination 
and remuneration, stakeholder relationship, 
governance, CSR and sustainability. 
For detailed roles and responsibilities of these 
Committees refer the Corporate Governance 
Report forming part of this Annual Report.

Ethics and Transparency
Guided by the Tata Group ethos, we are 
committed to following the highest standards 
of ethics, transparency, and fairness in all 
our dealings. Our governance framework 
is anchored in the Tata Code of Conduct, 
which drives responsible behaviour across all 
levels of the organisation. This framework is 
reinforced through adoption of the Tata Business 
Excellence Model and Policies on Anti ‑Bribery, 
Anti‑Corruption, Anti ‑Money Laundering, and 
Corporate Disclosure Practices. Our Board 
Members and Senior Management affirm 
their compliance to the Tata Code of Conduct 
annually underscoring the culture of integrity 
and accountability.
To promote a transparent and safe working 
environment, we have implemented a 
comprehensive Whistle Blower Policy and Vigil 
Mechanism framework that encourages reporting 
of unethical conduct without fear of retaliation. 
Employees and stakeholders have access to 
confidential channels, including an independent 
ethics helpline and secure email contacts, to 
raise concerns. All grievances, including those 
related to human rights and the prevention of 
sexual harassment, are addressed through 
a structured redressal process. We uphold 
a zero‑tolerance tolerance towards unethical 
practices and remain steadfast in safeguarding 
the rights and dignity of every individual we 
engage with.
Policies
We have formulated a comprehensive set of 
policies, rooted in the Tata Code with oversight by 
the Board, to facilitate informed decision ‑making 
and risk ‑conscious strategy for sustainable 
growth. These policies are regularly reviewed 
by the Board and are available on the Company 
website as well as internal platforms.Key Policies
 −Tata Code of Conduct (TCoC)
 −Anti‑Bribery and Anti ‑Corruption Policy
 −Anti‑Money Laundering Policy
 −Vigil Mechanism and 
Whistle Blower Policy
 −Prevention of Sexual Harassment Policy
 −Equal Opportunity Policy
 −Human Rights Policy
 −Health and Safety Policy
 −Environment and Sustainability Policy
 −Policy on Corporate Social Responsibility
 −Risk Management Policy
 −Cyber Security Governance Framework 
and Data Privacy Policy

 −Policy on Related Party Transactions

Disclosure Practices
 −Vendor Code of ConductRegulatory Compliance Framework
We have a robust process and dedicated 
Compliance Team to oversee the compliance with 
all applicable laws which impact the Company’s 
business. A web ‑based Statutory Compliance 
Monitoring Tool has been implemented to 
facilitate compliance monitoring. We have 
aligned this process as per the Compliance 
Risk Management framework prescribed by the 
COSO Framework (Committee of of Sponsoring 
Organizations of the Treadway Commission), 
2017. Over 320 applicable laws are mapped 
to over 2,100 internal users for ensuring 
regulatory compliance.
During FY25, we have not received any  
significant fines/penalties from any regulatory  
authorities.
Internal Control Framework
We have in place a robust internal control 
framework to protect the Company's assets and 
promote operational excellence. This system 
operates through clearly documented standard 
operating procedures, policies and process 
guidelines. Regular assessments of Risk and 
Control Matrix guide our actions relating to 
preventive and automated control progression.  
It provides a reasonable level of assurance 
regarding the achievement of our operational, 
compliance and reporting objectives. The design 
of this framework is well ‑structured and regularly 
reviewed to ensure its effectiveness and  
appropriateness.Customer Data Privacy and Security
In today’s digital ‑first world, protecting personal 
information is critical to running our business 
without disruptions, retaining customers, and 
safeguarding web ‑based brand and reputation.
Our Information Security Policy guides how we 
manage information assets, supported by a 
multi‑layered approach combining organisational, 
physical, and technological controls to protect 
sensitive data. Personal data is protected 
through privacy principles, regular audits, and 
compliance with applicable data protection 
regulations. A CISO ‑led Information Security 
Management System (ISMS) is in place to 
strengthen security through timely interventions, 
legal compliance, and regular reviews. 
A well‑defined Incident Management Policy, 
together with a cyber crisis plan and technology 
solutions, enables us to detect, respond to, and 
mitigate risks. Regular security audits, cyber 
security maturity assessments, and ongoing 
employee education help reinforce a strong 
culture of security and readiness across the 
organisation, aligned with the proposed Digital 
Personal Data Protection Act, 2023. During FY25, 
there were no incidents of cyber security or 
data breaches.

=== page 57 ===
Operating Environment and
Opportunities
The Indian economy continues to grow and is now over H330 Lakh Cr ($ 4 Tn) in 
GDP terms, on course to become the 3rd largest over the next few years. Globally, 
and in India, the operating environment was influenced by a variety of geopolitical 
developments, inflationary trends, and post-pandemic economic revitalisation. 
However, given the strengths of India’s demographic dividend, focus on rapid 
urbanisation, and the rise of a digital-first society, the country withstood the market 
turbulence quite well, with encouraging GDP growth forecasts for FY26. 
Rising Disposable Incomes
The steady increase in disposable incomes, 
particularly in India's urban and semi ‑urban 
regions, has led to a surge in discretionary 
spending. In the last five years, disposable 
income has risen by almost 50%.  
Demographic Impact
India’s growing young population and the 
rapid urbanisation continue to drive demand 
for trendy and affordable apparel. Millennials 
and Gen Z, as digital natives, seek seamless 
shopping experiences across physical and 
digital touchpoints. 
Growing Income Disparity
India’s income distribution continues to evolve, 
leading to varying demand patterns across 
consumer segments. According to the World 
Inequality Report 2022, the top 10% of the 
population accounts for 57% of national income, 
while the top 1% holds 22%. Meanwhile, the 
bottom 50% accounts for 13% of income. This 
growing diversity in spending power underscores 
the importance of offering both premium and 
value ‑led products, enabling us to serve a wider 
range of customers effectively. Changing Customer Preferences
Consumers across all age groups are 
increasingly driven by aspirational purchases 
and influenced by global fashion and lifestyle 
trends. Indian consumers are increasingly 
seeking rewarding value propositions, backed 
by convenience, personalisation, and ethical 
products. There is a marked shift towards 
expectations of great value, good quality, and 
contemporary fashion styles. Trends such as 
sustainability, gender‑neutral fashion, and 
support for local brands are influencing buying 
decisions. McKinsey’s State of the Consumer 
Report 2024  reveals that in advanced markets, 
over a third of consumers have tried different 
brands, and approximately 40% have switched 
retailers in search of better prices and discounts. 
36% of consumers plan to purchase private‑label 
products more frequently, and 60% believe 
private brands offer equal or better quality. 
While sustainability is a preferred choice among 
both Gen Z and millennials, very few are willing 
to pay a premium for such products.Competitive Landscape
Fashion Trends and Responsiveness
The fast fashion model is gaining traction across 
several brands, requiring rapid design ‑to‑shelf 
capabilities. Competitors, both domestic and 
global, are raising the bar in terms of speed, 
trend responsiveness, and pricing approach.
Digital transformation/Artificial 
Intelligence (AI)
The adoption of AI and digital tools is 
transforming retail — from demand forecasting 
and inventory management to personalised 
marketing. Players are leveraging data ‑
driven insights for customer engagement and 
operational efficiency. 
E-commerce
E‑commerce penetration in the lifestyle segment 
is expected to grow from 13% to 18 ‑22% by 
2028. Convenience, range and pricing points 
are key decision drivers propelling growth of 
online channels.  The omnichannel play offered 
by the e ‑commerce platforms can augment 
the overall scale and size for a retail player. 
As much as there are growth opportunities 
offered by e ‑commerce marketplaces, there 
are sustainability challenges too. For example, 
sales returns by online customers in the apparel 
segment are estimated to be around 25 ‑40%.
Geopolitical Conditions
Global uncertainties, such as tariff changes, 
trade disruptions, geopolitical tensions, and 
inflationary pressures from energy prices, can 
affect supply chains and sourcing costs.Climate Change and India’s Evolving 
ESG Landscape
The fashion retail sector is increasingly under 
scrutiny for its environmental impact. Changing 
weather patterns and regulatory pressures on 
carbon emissions may affect the production and 
distribution of products. Additionally, the ESG 
landscape in India is evolving rapidly, driven 
by regulatory and policy shifts, rising consumer 
demand for sustainable products, and the 
country’s net ‑zero goal. 
Dynamic Regulatory Landscape
The Indian retail industry is governed by a 
dynamic regulatory environment involving 
taxation, labour laws, FDI norms, and 
environmental regulations. 
Policies also promote sustainable practices 
through incentives for green processing, waste 
management, and technical textile development. 
With ESG integration, the fashion industry can 
significantly contribute to India’s Sustainable 
Development Goals, particularly in employment, 
gender equality, and climate action. 
Evolving data privacy laws, especially 
for e‑commerce operations, require 
compliance upgrades. 
Source: Industry Reports/internal analysis Source: Industry Reports/internal analysis

=== page 58 ===
Opportunities
India’s fashion and lifestyle sector continues to 
offer an encouraging growth driven by favourable 
demographics, digitalisation, rising disposable 
incomes, urbanisation and evolving consumer 
preferences. The market, valued at ~ H13 Lakh Cr 
in 2025, is projected to reach ~ H18 Lakh Cr by 
2028, growing at a CAGR of 10–12%.  
The market growth opportunity for organised 
retailers unfolds at two levels firstly, growth 
in India’s GDP would result in the growth of 
the apparel retail market size, and secondly, 
shifts from unorganised to organised retailers 
provide a constant impetus to the relevant 
growth trajectory. As one of the key players in 
the sector, these market growth opportunities 
would continue to favourably impact us as well.
The Indian consumer is increasingly seeking an 
aspirational product proposition, attractive pricing 
and importantly ready accessibility. We believe 
building density of our presence in key markets 
allows us proximity and the ability to service 
our customers readily. The market opportunity 
associated with building brands and a pure play 
direct ‑to‑customer business like ours remains 
immense. Further, having established and tested 
the business and operating processes at scale, 
expansion into underpenetrated tier 2/3 market 
presents a compelling runway. The traction 
from customers has been encouraging given 
the growing awareness of our brands across 
markets. We believe this play out augurs well 
as an indicator as we pursue the next phase of 
our growth.Our ability to leverage Trent’s Playbook to 
expand emerging formats and categories such 
as beauty and personal care, lingerie, innerwear 
and footwear would allow us to enhance the 
addressable market size. Launch of new formats 
such as Samoh in the premium occasion wear 
space, leveraging our back ‑end operating 
platform, gives us the ability to nurture and step 
into newer customer and product segments. 
Expansion of our Zudio format in the UAE market 
allows us to learn and improve our product 
value proposition and gives us the opportunity 
to establish ourselves in the international 
marketplace over time.
Our business model choices are unique and 
competitively differentiated. For example, 100% 
of own branded product offering, distribution 
through a network of only our own stores, a 
tightly integrated back ‑end operating platform 
and design, and buying ‑led product innovation 
capabilities make our business model relatively 
attractive and profitably scalable. Steady 
improvements in the ROCE and ROE profiles are 
key outcome indicators in this respect.
Our food & grocery offerings through our Star 
business, with a predominant focus on more than 
70% of our own branded product portfolio, allow 
us to expand our reach and penetrate deeper into 
the attractive food and grocery space.

=== page 59 ===
1. Identify and Analyse
Risks are identified and analysed for each 
business and support unit. Our management 
team is encouraged to express their views and 
perceptions of various risks impacting their 
specific  business/functional areas to cover a 
bottoms ‑up view. The Board, Audit Committee, 
and Risks Management Committee offer a holistic 
top‑down risk perspective.
2. Assess and Prioritise
Risk impact, likelihood, and velocity are assessed, 
keeping strategic objectives and business 
deliverables in mind. The probability and impact 
of the risks materialising are rated, considering the 
effectiveness of existing risk mitigation initiatives 
and controls.Risk Management
3. Develop Risk Response
Risk management/mitigation plans are developed 
in close alignment with the business objectives.
Mitigation action plans are developed by way of risk 
acceptance, mitigation, sharing, and transferring of 
the risk within risk appetite and risk tolerance levels.
4. Assess Risk Response
Our evolving business dynamics continually alter 
the profile of risks; therefore, ongoing reviews and 
conversations about the risks allow risk owners 
and management to assess the effectiveness 
of identified mitigation action plans. Appropriate 
measures are undertaken to strengthen the 
coverage and effectiveness of identified risk controls 
and mitigation action plans.
5. Monitor, Communicate and Report
The management and leadership team is 
engaged regularly in assessing the Company's 
risk profile and the effectiveness of the risk 
management programme.We follow a structured Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) approach integrated 
with our planning and performance management processes. Our ERM practices, 
aligned with the COSO Framework (Committee of Sponsoring Organisations of the 
Treadway Commission) 2017, strengthen processes around governance, strategy, 
objective -setting, and day-to-day operations. By linking strategy and business 
objectives with risk-assessed decision-making, this approach enhances performance 
and cultivates a risk-aware and compliance-driven culture.
Risk Management Process
Risk Governance
The Risk Management Committee, Audit Committee, and the Board are periodically updated on key 
risks and any significant changes in the Company’s risk profile. A Central Risk Management Team 
under the leadership of the Chief Financial Officer facilitates execution of the Risk Management 
Practices in the Company. Regular communication initiatives and risk workshops within the Company 
strengthen risk awareness, engagement, and reporting. The deployment of technology and analytical 
tools makes the risk monitoring, communication, and reporting process more efficient.

KEY RISKS MITIGATION MEASURES
Business Environment Risk: Adapting to Market and Trends
Being on trend is the centrepiece of our business. Any 
inability to adapt to market fashion trends and respond to 
changes in consumer expectations is an inherent risk we 
face. Growing competition and attractiveness of the industry, 
along with technological innovation, further pose challenges 
to the business on various fronts. Curating the retail space, 
offerings, and display while keeping in mind the micro‑markets, 
demographics, and the needs/convenience of the consumers 
adds to the complexities involved and significantly impacts 
delivered margins. −We continuously monitor, track the market and 
competitive landscape and interact with customers to 
understand their needs.
 −We undertake several initiatives to spot emerging fashion 
trends. Our continuous focus on building product design 
capabilities allows us to keep the product portfolio and 
brand offerings refreshed.
 −We strive to identify and incubate growth drivers and 
enablers to deliver business results.
 −Tailoring space management algorithms in cognisance of 
regional and local variations.
 −Focus on margins, cost efficiencies, quality, and 
compliance to drive a sustainable business while meeting 
evolving consumer expectations.
Customer Acquisition, Retention and Experience
Customer acquisition refers to attracting new customers. 
Customer retention is focused on developing better relationships 
with existing customers to increase loyalty and drive repeat 
purchases. Customer experience represents the sum of how 
customers feel about our brand, the experience they have with 
our products, our stores, and their interactions with our brands 
at every point of their journey. Customer retention is critical 
because the cost of acquiring new customers is much higher 
than that of retaining existing customers. Customer experience 
influences customer retention. −Customer interactions and inputs are routinely reviewed 
and actioned.
 −Listening posts, capture customers’ feedback and inputs 
on an ongoing basis.
 −Store upgrades, rigorous training of store staff, and 
continuous digital improvements have been our 
primary focus for enhancing the customer experience 
and engagement at various touchpoints both in‑store 
and online.
Data Risk: Information and Cybersecurity
Increased reliance on digital systems raises the importance of 
cybersecurity. Possible impacts include the loss of customer 
data, business interruptions, potential fines, and reputational 
damage. −Our business and IT systems are continually upgraded to 
mitigate cybersecurity and data protection risks. We are 
also ISO 27001 certified, which ensures that information 
security management system controls are in place and 
enforced. We continue to align ourself with the emerging 
and evolving requirements under the DPDP Act and 
IT Act.
 −Robust business continuity plans ensure that all 
systems necessary to manage operations are active 
and functional.
 −Regular independent third ‑party and Tata Group‑level 
assessments of data and cybersecurity enable 
us to strengthen and enhance our IT systems and 
network environment.Key Market Risks and Mitigation Plan
The Risk Management Framework identifies both internal and external risks, including strategic, 
compliance, financial, operational, competition, ESG, information technology, and cybersecurity risks.
KEY RISKS MITIGATION MEASURES
Digital and Technology Risk: E-commerce, Social Media and 
Business Operations
Digital advancements and associated technology play an 
important role in managing the portfolio of growing businesses 
efficiently and effectively. Customers are increasingly looking at 
e‑commerce as a convenient channel for shopping. Brands are 
required to fulfill this expectation with the promise of offering a 
frictionless purchasing journey by adopting digital interventions.
The agility in adopting the rapidly evolving generative AI and 
machine learning tools and solutions has a critical impact on 
business strategy and operations. −We actively pursue initiatives to strengthen the digital and 
technological capability and scalability of our business. 
An omnichannel ‑focused approach for Westside to 
integrate our online and physical stores is a step in 
this direction.
 −The adoption of contemporary platforms, digital tools and 
technology for enhancing customer experience, process 
effectiveness and operational productivity remains a 
key goal.
Talent Risk: Capabilities and Succession Planning
Increased competitive intensity, coupled with the sector’s 
opportunities, has created dissonance in talent demand and 
supply. Being a fashion‑led business model, we need to attract 
and retain a pool of creative talent across product categories. 
Most organisations are revisiting capability and capacity 
requirements for newer business avenues. The emergence 
of start‑ups and PE/IPO‑led funding of new ventures has 
created deep pockets for select entities. Almost all industries 
are confronted with talent attrition. The cost of talent is also on 
the rise. −We recognise that the primary way to mitigate the 
risk of attrition and attract fresh talent is to foster a 
healthy workplace culture and provide colleagues with 
development opportunities. We adopt a multi‑pronged 
approach (the triad of Education, Exposure and 
Experience) to identify critical roles and build 
succession plans.
 −We provide a fair and equal working environment with the 
Tata Code of Conduct as our cultural anchor. Respect, 
dignity, and ethical conduct are the cornerstones of our 
organisational culture.
 −We also adopt appropriate communication and feedback 
platforms, including external surveys like Employee 
Satisfaction Survey and Leadership in Business Ethics for 
continuous improvement and succession planning, along 
with ongoing investment in learning and development 
initiatives for our employees.
Scalability Risk
Limited availability of quality real estate, coupled with high 
rentals and non‑adherence to committed schedules by 
developers, pose significant challenges to the deployment of 
strategic plans relating to expansion. Rapid expansion also 
entails scalability risks in areas of sourcing and supply chain 
capabilities.
Optimised product inventory management to balance desired 
availability and overstocking risk. −A rigorous property selection process through multiple 
filters applied to store quality and economics enables 
us to expand sustainably. Continuous monitoring of key 
performance indicators on upcoming projects helps us 
retain visibility on the delivery of store locations.
 −Increasing focus on agile and dependable sourcing, 
merchandising, cost efficiencies, and quality and 
compliance across the supply chain and backend 
readiness are integral to our growth plans.
 −Disciplined inventory planning, including agility for 
real‑time course corrections.
 −Deployment of state ‑of‑the‑art technology and digital 
tools to support scalable and sustained growth across 
the supply chain.

KEY RISKS MITIGATION MEASURES
ESG Risk
ESG exposures comprise environmental, social, and 
governance‑related risks. We have emphasised and adopted 
pragmatic practices to address ESG‑related objectives. 
Nevertheless, we recognise that this is an ongoing journey and 
involves a range of risks that need to be mitigated. −Initiatives under the sustainability agenda have been 
identified for mitigating environmental impact and have 
been integrated with our strategy.
 −We adopt the Tata Code of Conduct, which facilitates 
a fair working environment and appropriate behaviour 
by employees. We also work closely with our vendors 
to ensure they remain compliant with the Trent Code of 
Conduct while ensuring regulatory and social compliance 
and respect for human rights at their premises.
 −We lead with responsible business conduct and 
concurrently address ESG objectives as enshrined 
under the National Guidelines for Responsible Business 
Conduct (NGRBC), and have also included details in our 
BRSR Report, which is a part of this Annual Report.
Partnership Risk: JVs and Associates
We have multiple alliances, including with Tesco PLC, Inditex, 
and MAS Group. These alliances may entail certain risks, 
including with respect to continuity. The associations with Inditex 
are dependent on the majority partner for permission to use the 
said brands in India, subject to its terms & specifications. Also, 
the entire control over core customer propositions is with Inditex 
for those JVs. −We have sought to build on the respective relationships 
and leverage the learnings across concepts to the extent 
possible/relevant.
 −In the case of our association with Inditex, we continue 
to view the said commitments primarily as a financial 
investment and are cognisant of related operating 
and strategic uncertainties, including with respect to 
continuity, given the nature of the arrangements.
Reputation Risk
Our products and brands need to comply with quality standards 
and product regulations to ensure there are no material product 
liability claims and lawsuits for infringement. Non‑adherence to 
local laws or international laws and unethical or irresponsible 
behaviour at our locations and by any vendor would result in a 
negative impact on our reputation. We also need to be vigilant 
for cyber ‑attacks or potential data leakage and the impact of 
social media virality. −We carry out regular assessments of product quality 
and safety. Regulatory compliance by vendors/SMETA 
(Sedex Members Ethical Trade Audit) annual audit is part 
of vendor onboarding and continuous review.
 −A comprehensive regulatory compliance oversight 
framework aligned with the COSO guidelines has been 
implemented across our operations.
 −A proactive approach is taken to mitigate cyber 
risks with a focus on training, monitoring, and 
regular communication.
 −Public Relations management with accurate, transparent, 
timely, and reliable communication to prevent reputational 
risk and mitigate consequences is key to managing 
this risk.

=== page 63 ===
We are culturally wired to encourage creativity aimed at delivering 
compelling offerings. Strong product disciplines across the value chain 
are a defining feature of our playbook. This includes ownership of 
product design and curation, focus on speed of concept to market and 
consistency of offer across channels.Consistently introducing on-trend offerings with a compelling value 
proposition, coupled with control of our consumer touchpoints, 
differentiates our brands. Customers subconsciously attach more 
value to full-priced offerings and corelate full price to a strong brand. 
Consequently, we stay away from discounting and also avoid active 
marketing spends. We instead focus on being accessible and building 
a critical mass of presence to drive awareness. This model facilitates 
a strong organic connection with our customers while at the same time 
affording better economics.

=== page 64 ===
Our model involving our own brands and direct-to-consumer 
distribution, affords us the ability to integrally define and control the 
sequence, timing and quantum of allocation/ replenishment of our 
offerings across channels and geographies. We believe, in lifestyle 
retailing, the presentation outcomes are material and the allocation/
replenishment model is integral to solving this puzzle together with the 
consumer moment of truth availability.
We seek to actively own and curate the consumer image of our brands. 
In many ways Trent is quintessentially direct-to-consumer as we adopt 
direct distribution and eschew third-party channels and intermediation of 
any kind. We adopt a deeply integrated approach to our stores and digital 
channels. Consequently, we command best-in-class, full-priced revenues, 
consistency of proposition across channels, as well as industry-leading 
online return rates. This playbook affords us differentiated disciplines, 
allowing complete ownership over multiple parts of our value chain from 
sourcing and production facilities to retailing. On the other hand, this 
approach has also meant a more calibrated playout of scale. As we have 
gained critical mass with our fashion brands, we now see a growing 
flywheel of market traction allowing us to accelerate growth.

Trent has evolved into a platform that allows us to originate, incubate

We are increasingly expanding our presence across more diverse markets

across comparative micro-markets vis-à-vis just the performance of 
comparative stores.

Over time, we seek to build out a compelling and future-fit portfolio of

addressing a substantial market opportunity. As part of this agenda, we 
are strengthening our core to drive efficiencies, enhancing our product 
proposition in select categories and driving premiumisation by way of 
desirable products, convenience and experience.
Given the scale, the complexities, the rapidly evolving consumer context 
and our growth ambitions, we are committing substantial investments in 
technology, automation and supply chains. For instance, across the fashion 
portfolio, we have transitioned to universal RFID tagging and tracking of 
merchandise. Our current operations already leverage numerous use cases 
from a diverse set of interventions.
We see the imperative for leveraging multiple technology interventions 
(including in AI/ML-led augmentation for design, delivery and landing 
of consumer outcomes), as well as the exciting possibilities of realising 
disproportionate resets across a spectrum of areas in the near to medium 
term. This is particularly exciting given that we exercise complete control 
over the entire value chain that is afforded by our business model.

Materiality
A structured materiality assessment, conducted through comprehensive engagement 
with both internal and external stakeholders, enables us to gain meaningful insights 
into the economic, environmental, social, and governance (ESG) issues most critical 
to our stakeholders and most impactful for our long-term growth.
Materiality Assessment
We conducted our first materiality assessment in FY23, engaging with 47 internal and 220 external 
stakeholders. This process helped us identify six key sustainability topics that became the key drivers 
to align our business objectives and stakeholder expectations. Our materiality process follows the 
Indian regulatory framework (NGRBC) and the Global Reporting Initiative (GRI) framework.

=== page 77 ===
Innovation and Design
We constantly adopt market leading technology solutions and innovative 
interventions across our value chain to meet the evolving needs of business 
and aspirations of our stakeholders. By integrating technology across our core 
operations — from supply chain to store execution, we have derived significant 
efficiencies in tandem with our growth trajectory.Innovation to Enhance Operational 
Efficiency 
We constantly reimagine our tech stack and 
adopt technologies that allow for increased 
efficiency, visibility, and scale.
Next-gen Retail: Radio-Frequency 
Identification (RFID) in Motion
We continue to build a future ‑proof portfolio of 
lifestyle brands, enhancing operational efficiency 
with RFID technology. This streamlines inventory 
management, improves tracking, reduces manual 
tagging, and boosts store operations. Our fashion 
brands now use RFID for merchandise tracking, 
significantly improving supply chain management 
and unlocking new uses in distribution centres 
and stores. Faster 'box ‑to‑floor' processes 
also increase product availability. For our store 
colleagues, RFID adoption has simplified routine 
tasks, improved visibility, and freed up time to 
focus more on customer engagement. Future 
plans include automated stocktakes and faster 
resolution of out ‑of‑stock issues. RFID checkouts 
is also being rolled out in a phased manner.
Warehouse Automation
We are investing in modernising our backend 
infrastructure and logistics network to ensure agility 
and resilience in the face of accelerated retail 
growth and evolving customer demands, prioritising 
faster delivery times, wider product availability, and 
a seamless shopping experience.  Towards this, we have advanced a series of 
warehouse automation initiatives, including the 
deployment of spiral conveyors, boom conveyors, 
and cross ‑belt sorters. These solutions have 
considerably increased our throughput capacity 
and reduced manual efforts in key processes like 
sorting, loading, and unloading. Automation has 
also improved space utilisation and allowed us to 
scale efficiently to meet peak demand periods. 
These initiatives also enhance productivity and 
reduce cost impact.
Transport Management System (TMS)
We successfully implemented TMS to bring more 
visibility and control over our logistics operations. 
The TMS enables route optimisation, real ‑time 
shipment tracking, load consolidation, and 
performance monitoring of transport partners. 
This has led to improved on ‑time deliveries, 
reduced logistics costs, and enhanced internal 
customer satisfaction.
RISE with SAP 
We have migrated to RISE with SAP to transition 
from the traditional on ‑premise systems to 
a cloud ‑first model with an aim to improve 
operational efficiency and agility as well as 
accelerate customer ‑facing innovations. The 
new solution unifies applications, platforms, 
and infrastructure. These services give us 
access to a scalable, cloud ‑based infrastructure 
that simplifies our IT operations and creates 
opportunities for further refinements.

=== page 78 ===
Innovation and Design for  
Product Excellence 
We encourage a culture of creativity to deliver 
products that connect with our customers. Our 
approach is built on ensuring product discipline 
across the product value chain — from design 
to curation so we can translate concepts to 
products on the shelf efficiently and maintain 
consistency across all channels.
At the heart of our offerings is a dedicated in ‑
house creative team that designs and develops 
our entire product portfolio, be it fashion, lifestyle, 
or home décor. The design teams undertake 
research and analyse market trends so we offer 
customers fresh and exciting products.
Fashion-forward Design
Our captive design studios provide insights into 
the latest fashion trends and translates them into 
product designs. This capability strengthens the 
brand's market position with inspiration from high 
fashion markets. 
The use of AI and data analytics in the design 
process helps create innovative products through 
Generative	AI	(GenAI).	Insights	on	coloūur, 	
silhouettes, and patterns, along with weekly trading 
and fabric analysis, enable the design team to 
produce relevant and market‑driven offerings.
Digital, Data and Analytics Centre  
of Excellence
During the year, we set up a Digital, Data and 
Analytics Centre of Excellence. The centre 
focuses on developing actionable diagnostic 
insights that cater to business outcome, enabling 
informed decision ‑making. By integrating 
predictive analytics into our key processes, we are able provide insights that empower our 
design and buying teams to respond effectively 
to evolving customer needs. Generative AI use 
cases have been deployed across a few business 
processes with encouraging results.
We are also actively attracting talent with 
advanced data science and related expertise and 
upskilling our existing workforce through targeted 
Learning & Development (L&D) programmes 
to enhance our ability to rapidly respond to the 
changing market trends.
Technology-Powered Sustainable Retail  
Internet of Things (IoT) systems have been 
deployed in a substantial number of stores to 
enable real ‑time data monitoring and support 
energy ‑efficient operations. Considering the 
baseline, early pilots have shown energy savings 
while enhancing the consistency and comfort of 
the in ‑store environment.
Inverter air conditioning systems have been 
installed to provide efficient cooling and further 
energy savings. Uniform LED lighting has been 
implemented across all stores, distribution 
centres and office locations to reduce energy 
consumption and minimise environmental impact. 
Intelligent electricity control systems have been 
integrated to optimise energy usage, reduce 
wastage, and improve operational efficiency. 
These systems automatically adjust based on 
usage patterns, contributing to more sustainable 
and cost ‑effective store management.

Expanding Reach
We are expanding across formats, locations, and channels. For us, growth also means 
staying closer to our customers. As their needs and preferences change, our brands 
stay flexible — creating seamless shopping experiences.Store portfolio review for brand congruence and 
consequential consolidation and enhancement 
initiatives remains an integral component of our 
strategy. We believe that our stores, in addition to 
being a venue to sell our products, also give us a 
direct connection to our customers. In FY25, we 
added 40 new stores and consolidated 24 stores. 
While store expansion is a key growth lever for us, 
maintaining the quality and physical aesthetics 
of stores and ensuring consistent customer 
experience is equally important to us.
Zudio
Growing Footprint
During the year, Zudio added 242 new stores 
and consolidated 24. With a store footprint of 
around 7,000 ‑12,000 sq. ft., the concept enables 
expansion across numerous micro ‑markets.
Zudio International:  The brand made its 
international debut with its first store in Dubai, 
an opportunity to test its value ‑driven fashion 
proposition in a diverse and fashion ‑forward 
market. The learnings we will gain by competing 
with international brands in the UAE will help 
us further improve our competitive edge back 
in India.Retail Expansion  
From our first store in 1998 to this moment, 
our journey has been defined by growth, 
transformation, and a lasting connection with 
millions of customers. We use customer insights 
to improve our products, stores, and services. 
Our exclusive portfolio of in ‑house brands is 
thoughtfully developed to meet diverse customer 
lifestyles and aspirations, ranging from everyday 
essentials to fashion ‑forward collections  — each 
with a distinct, trend ‑driven identity and value 
proposition. We continue to invest in highly 
visible store locations with contemporary design, 
curated storytelling, and easy navigation.
Westside
Highly Prominent and Contemporary 
Lifestyle Store Experience
We increasingly seek to grow a portfolio 
of prominent stores that have significant 
street presence in marquee locations with 
a footprint predominantly between 20,000 
‑30,000 sq.ft. While we pursue a sustainable 
store expansion agenda, we also conduct an 
active store optimisation programme which 
involves identifying brand ‑diluting stores and 
consolidating or upgrading them with newer 
stores in more attractive micro ‑markets.

Comfortable and Sustainable Store 
Environments 
We ensure our stores remain comfortable and 
efficient through regular maintenance. The use of 
energy ‑efficient inverter ‑based air conditioning 
provides uninterrupted cooling while reducing 
energy use and carbon emissions.
Connected and Localised Operations  
Our operations are supported by a network 
of distribution centres, sourcing offices, and 
corporate offices. We follow a decentralised 
distribution model, which helps us meet regional 
demand more quickly and reduce lead times. 
Our sourcing offices are located close to key 
vendor hubs, allowing better coordination 
with suppliers. Around 90% of our sourcing is 
done within the country, which helps speed up 
procurement and reduces dependencies.
Teams at our corporate offices support the 
business through planning, coordination, and 
oversight to run operations smoothly and respond 
better to customers.

Our Employees
Being people-conscious is rooted in the Tata values and the Tata Code of Conduct, and 
is a strategic pillar of our resilience agenda. Engaged and empowered employees help 
us build brands that are fresh, responsible, and bring joy to all.Attracting and Retaining Talent
Attracting, developing, and retaining the right 
talent is a key to shaping an agile and responsive 
organisation. We hire fresh talent from campuses 
across India and professionals who bring the 
right balance of skills, expertise, and experience 
to our business.
Trent Home Grown program offers fashion school 
graduates year ‑long hands ‑on training in buying, 
merchandising, sourcing, and quality, followed 
by placement in roles based on their skills 
and interests.
As we continue to expand our operations 
and reach in new markets, our workforce 
grows in tandem, bringing diverse talents and 
perspectives to our organisation. We have 
employed over 27,800 people in FY25.
While attracting the right talent is crucial to 
building a successful organisation, talent 
retention and attrition play a pivotal role, with 
attrition serving as an indicator of employees’ 
relationship with the Company. We undertake 
measures to improve the overall working 
environment, expand professional development 
opportunities, and stay engaged with our 
employees to manage attrition. The employee 
turnover has reduced to 53% from 67% in the 
previous year. The new addition to the Trent 
family not only contribute to our dynamic and 
inclusive work environment but also play a vital 
role in driving our strategic initiatives forward.
Learning and Development
Training and upskilling of our employees is critical 
to ensure they have the capabilities to improve 
productivity, innovate, and grow our business 
sustainably. We also invest in leadership 
development and future ‑ready skills to strengthen 
our internal talent pipeline.
Our Learning and Development (L&D) strategy 
is focused on enhancing critical functional skills and addressing specific business requirements. 
This is supported by key leadership development 
programmes in collaboration with Tata Group 
teams, alongside tailored programs implemented 
across various functions to meet their 
unique needs.
Align: Learning is tailored to meet individual, 
functional and organisational priorities, driven by 
personal development plans.
Equip: Employees are empowered to take charge 
of their learning, with availability of online and 
mobile based learning technologies. e.g., Seekho 
App and Z Learn.
Sustain: Learning is supported with post works, 
action learning projects and other means to sustain  
the learnings for maximum impact.
Diversity, Equity, Inclusion (DEI)
Our dedication to nurture diversity within the 
Company begins at the highest levels. While over 
one‑third of the Board of Directors and 34% of 
management level employees comprise women, 
we continue to work to improve gender diversity 
throughout the organisation.

towards creating diverse workplace and tap

Performance Management
Our performance management framework 
addresses the needs of individual growth and 
encourages employees to be the best they can 
be. It combines competency ‑based assessments 
with personalised development plans so 
employees can build on their strengths and grow 
professionally. Employee bonuses are linked to the 
Company’s financial and business performance 
to create a shared sense of responsibility and 
success. We follow ‘pay for performance’ 
approach in our compensation policy which 
supports merit and performance based 
differentiation. Performance and compensation 
review for Key Managerial Personnels (KMPs) and 
Senior Management Personnel is overseen by the 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee. 
The system uses a three ‑pronged approach:  
 −Aligning individual goals with Trent’s 
strategic priorities
 −Fostering a culture of continuous feedback 
and support
 −Recognising and rewarding performance to

Our employees can share their experiences 
through feedback tools, including external

shared by our employees are used to refine 
people strategies, workplace practices, talent 
development, and succession strategies.
In the most recent Employee Satisfaction Survey 
2024, majority of our employees reaffirmed Trent 
as a great place to work. Over the years, we have 
consistently maintained a response rate of over 
89%. Our scores rank among the highest in the 
retail industry for three consecutive years. 
Human Rights   
For us, fostering a safe environment 
encompasses providing employees and business 
partners with the opportunity to voice concerns 
and grievances related to the workplace and 
business practices. Emphasising transparent 
communication, we encourage our people to 
raise and discuss concerns with their Functional 
Heads, Human Resources Team, or designated 
ethics officials.
We respect human rights across our operations 
and value chain, guided by the Universal 
Declaration of Human Rights and the Tata Code 
of Conduct. 
Employee Health, Well-being and Safety 
Our Health and Safety (H&S) Policy and Practices 
are aligned with Tata Group Retail Safety 
Standards and statutory regulations such as the 
Shops and Establishment Act and the National 
Building Code for commercial spaces. The 
Policy also incorporates industry best practices 
recommended by safety experts and the Tata 
Group’s centralised safety team. 
Safety Governance
A key governance mechanism is the monthly 
Safety Committee Meeting, chaired by the 
Managing Director. These meetings serve as 
a platform to review safety performance, track 
progress on audit closures, and discuss strategic 
initiatives to enhance workplace safety.A robust Health and Safety Management System 
has been instituted, enabling proactive risk 
identification through the Hazard Identification 
and Risk Assessment (HIRA) methodology. 
A specialised cross ‑functional team—comprising 
safety officers, maintenance personnel, and 
trained volunteers—conducts HIRA  assessments 
at all locations.
Safety Awareness and Training
Safety training programmes are conducted for all 
our employees as a part of the induction process 
as well as e ‑learning platforms. 
Occupational Health Services and Well-being  
Employee health & safety and well ‑being is 
central to our people practices, with a focus on 
both mental and physical health.  
Physical wellness measures: 
 −On‑site Medical Support
 −Health Awareness Programmes
 −Fitness for All Programme 
We also conduct routine activities such as air and 
water quality checks, ergonomic assessments, 
regular servicing of air conditioning systems, and 
monitoring of light levels to comply with health 
and safety regulations.
For any mental health issues, employees as 
well as their families can access professional 
counselling and mental wellness support through 
a trusted third ‑party agency.
Health awareness: We conduct various initiatives 
at our work premises to promote health through 
awareness e.g., talks by dieticians on healthy 
eating practices.
Fitness Programmes: We promote employee 
ftness through various physical initiatives, such 
as ‘Fit and Fun’, which features various activities 
scheduled throughout the year according to an 
annual calendar.

Education
In partnership with the Karta Initiative India 
Foundation, the ‘Trent Scholar’ programme 
supports 50 Trent Scholars from highly 
marginalised backgrounds with an opportunity to 
pursue higher education in premier universities. 
Trent Scholars are supported in pursuing 
academics alongside internship, mentorship, and 
pastoral support. Over and above addressing 
pressing issues like diversity and inclusion, the 
programme also equips the Trent Scholars to 
realise their potential and be role models for 
their communities.
Women’s Education
We support the ‘Nanhi Kali’ project in partnership 
with K.C. Mahindra Education Trust. Designed 
to support girls from low ‑income families 
to complete formal schooling till Grade 10, 
the project has impacted 300 young girls in 
government schools in Mumbai, Maharashtra. 
While educational support is a key component 
of Nanhi Kali, the project also equips the young 
girls with 21st ‑century skills and offers holistic 
development through physical education.
To impact the status of India’s 
socio‑economic fabric, our CSR team 
designs social projects basis a need 
assessment, with a purposive approach 
to positively affirm marginalised persons 
especially youth and women from 
socially and economically disadvantaged 
backgrounds. Volunteering is one of the 
key mechanisms that we have chosen to 
channelise our most valuable assets, our 
people, towards societal good.Enriching Lives
Inspired by the Tata Group’s founding philosophy of giving back to society, we strive 
to empower our communities by broadening opportunities for them that contribute to 
long-term societal transformation.
Supplier Evaluation and Screening
Building supplier capabilities and ensuring 
alignment with our ethical standards strengthens 
supply continuity, quality, and long ‑term 
partnership value. Our suppliers are evaluated 
on their ability to meet business needs and 
uphold our values. Over 90% of our product 
vendors underwent SEDEX (Supplier Ethical 
Data Exchange) SMETA (Sedex Members 
Ethical Trade Audits) 4 ‑pillar audits covering 
labour standards, health & safety standards, 
environment standards, and business ethics, 
including human rights.
We evaluate new and existing suppliers against 
specific environmental and social criteria to 
identify improvement areas and build supplier 
capability through structured feedback, audit 
documentation, and ongoing support from 
our team.
   Labour Standards:  This pillar assesses 
compliance with labour laws and ethical 
standards, including working hours, 
wages, child labour, forced labour, and 
freedom of association. The aim is to 
ensure fair treatment and safe working 
conditions for all employees.
   Health and Safety Standards:  This 
pillar evaluates workplace safety and 
health practices. It  examines measures to 
prevent accidents and illness, emergency 
preparedness, and the overall health and 
well‑being of workers.   Environmental Standards:  This pillar 
reviews the environmental impact 
of business operations. It looks at 
waste management, resource use, 
pollution control, and adherence to 
environmental regulations, promoting 
sustainable practices.
   Business Ethics:  This pillar examines the 
ethical conduct of business operations, 
focusing on anti ‑corruption measures, 
transparency, and fair business 
practices. It aims to ensure integrity and 
accountability in business dealings.
Capability Building
We engage with our suppliers to support them 
in enhancing operational efficiencies, making 
informed fabric selections, ensuring social and 
environmental compliance, and leveraging 
technology to oversee production and quality 
milestones. Towards this, we have implemented 
Trent Vendor Code of Conduct for uniform 
adoption by the suppliers.
Human Rights across the Value Chain
We uphold human rights across our operations 
and value chain aligned with globally 
acknowledged frameworks. The Tata Code 
of Conduct and Trent’s Vendor Code of 
Conduct prohibit child, forced, or compulsory 
labour and any form of abuse or harassment 
while respecting collective bargaining and 
decent working conditions for workers.Responsible Supply Chain 
Responsible sourcing and supply chain practices strengthen our ability to anticipate 
and respond with agility to the changing needs of our customers, run disruption-free 
operations and grow business by delivering desirable products on time.

Entrepreneurship
Kaarigar Clinic, India’s first rural business clinic, 
helps skilled craftswomen from Kutch start and 
grow a sustainable business while enhancing 
social cohesion. Women leaders are nurtured 
and supported with business training, digital and 
design upskilling, as well as market linkages, to 
provide them platforms for entrepreneurship and 
sustainable livelihoods. The ‘Crafting a Better 
Planet’ initiative through its Plastic Bank, teaches 
women to collect plastic, that is then woven, 
upcycled, and curated into sustainable products, 
sold online and at exhibitions. Dissemination 
of the environmental and social impact of this 
programme is facilitated through women ‑led 
workshops held in schools.
Over the years, our goal has been to empower 
1,125 women in Kutch while upcycling over 
1.2 Million plastic bags — creating livelihoods 
while contributing to a cleaner planet.
Employability
In partnership with the Salaam Bombay 
Foundation, we support the skills@school 
programme that offers training in diverse fields 
such as Beauty & Wellness, Mobile Repair, 
Robotics, and Fashion Design, with curriculums aligned to the National Skills Qualification 
Framework (NSQF) of the National Skills 
Development Corporation (NSDC). Our project 
supported 363 students from Municipal and 
Government ‑aided schools in Mumbai and Pune. 
Financial literacy is also included in the training 
to enable trainees to navigate the financial 
dimension in the work they may take up.
Nutrition

circularity. Each purchase funds mid ‑day meals

environmental and social impact.

Resource Efficiency
Even as we expand our footprint, we continually explore avenues for preserving 
natural resources and managing our energy use, emissions, and waste to meet the 
rising demand for sustainable brands and products.Managing Environmental Impact 
Our focus towards being resource efficient 
lies in minimising energy consumption, 
augmenting our utilisation of renewable energy 
sources, diminishing our carbon footprint, and 
effectively managing waste generated through 
our operations. The demand for energy in our 
operations primarily arises from the needs for 
lighting, cooling, and connected load.
Energy Consumption and Emission
Our energy consumption and emission 
generation were 949 TJ and 189 kT in FY25, 
compared to 657 TJ and 123 kT in FY24, 
respectively. The increase is mainly on account 
of addition of new stores and expansion of 
existing stores. Additionally, rise in footprints 
and capacity enhancement at stores have 
also added to the energy consumption.  
Managing Energy Consumption and 
Emissions
We remain committed to renewable energy 
and enhancing energy efficiency across our 
value chain including our own operations and 
logistics activities. Over 40% of the energy used 
in our three distribution centres is sourced from 
rooftop solar installations. At the store level, 
initiatives such as the installation of LED lighting, 
Dx inverter air conditioners, and Internet of 
Things (IoT) based monitoring systems are being 
implemented to drive greater energy efficiency. 
Considering the baseline, early pilots have shown 
energy savings while enhancing the consistency 
and comfort of the in ‑store environment.
Packaging and Waste Management
The majority of our waste, which is 
non‑hazardous and consists of plastics, 
cardboard, fabric, and metal is generated from 
logistics, distribution centres, and retail stores. 
We generate a smaller volume of electronic 
waste through equipment and systems used 
in our  operations.Managing Waste
We generated 657 T plastic waste in FY25, which 
is 18% lesser than FY24 plastic waste generation 
(801 T). Shifting to more sustainable alternatives 
is reducing our reliance on plastic and cardboard 
used in apparel packaging. A centralised waste 
system ensures traceability, proper segregation, 
and compliant disposal. E ‑waste goes to 
authorised vendors; non ‑hazardous waste 
is recycled or responsibly managed through 
authorised vendors.
We have initiated use of recyclable totes instead 
of cardboard boxes that will reduce the waste 
generation within the Company.
Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR)
We comply with EPR regulations as a registered 
Brand Owner and Importer with Central Pollution 
Control Board through certified vendors who 
collect and recycle it into biodegradable bags, 
recycled cardboard, and paper — materials 
we then source for our use. Rice ‑paper bags 
have been introduced as an alternative to LDPE 
plastic, and our products now feature recycled 
paper tags to reduce environmental impact.
Water Management
As a retail fashion business, our water use 
is limited to human consumption at offices, 
stores, and distribution centers, sourced mainly 
from suppliers and groundwater. In FY25, 
we consumed 585 ML of water which was 477 ML 
in FY24. Though usage is low, we stay mindful 
and actively work to conserve water.

Pursuant to the changes made under the Income-Tax Act, 
1961, by the Finance Act, 2020, dividend paid or distributed 
by the Company shall be taxable in the hands of the 
shareholders. As a result, the Company will pay the Dividend 
after deducting tax at the source at applicable rates.
The Dividend, subject to the approval of shareholders at the 
ensuing AGM will be paid to the Members whose names 
appear in the Register of Members as on the Record date 
fixed by the Company i.e. Thursday, 12th June 2025, subject 
to deduction of tax at source, as applicable, on or after 
Monday, 7th July 2025.
Transfer to General Reserve
The Board has decided to retain the entire amount of Profit for 
FY 2024-25 in the Statement of Profit and Loss.
Share Capital
During the year, there was no change in the authorised, issued, 
subscribed, and paid-up share capital of the Company.
Material Changes and Commitments, if any, affecting 
the financial position of the Company
There have been no material changes or commitments 
affecting the financial position of the Company between the 
end of the FY 2024-25 and the date of this Report. There has 
been no change in the business of the Company.
Consolidated Financial Statements
As required under the SEBI Listing Regulations, the 
Consolidated Financial Statements prepared as per the 
Indian Accounting Standards (‘Ind AS’), form part of this 
Annual Report.
Performance Overview
Standalone Performance
During the year under review, the Company registered 
encouraging growth, serving as a reaffirmation of its strategic 
choices and pillars. Consistent delivery of compelling value 
proposition to the customers through a portfolio of brands 
and products owned by the Company remains at the core of 
the Company’s strategy. The Company’s operating discipline, differentiated business model coupled with consistent focus 
on the speed of execution continues to support its growth and 
expansion agenda.
Revenue from Operations for FY 2024-25 is ` 16,668.11 
Crore as against ` 11,926.56 Crore in FY 2023-24 registering 
a growth of 39.76%. Profit After Tax is ` 1,584.84 Crore in  
FY 2024-25 in comparison to 1,435.82 Crore in FY 2023-24. 
Total Comprehensive Income is ` 1,580.97 Crore in FY 2024-
25 as against ` 1,445.46 Crore in FY 2023-24. Profit After Tax 
and Total Comprehensive Income for FY 2023-24 included an 
Exceptional Gain of ` 406.60 Crore (net of tax) on account 
of reassessment of the estimates of measurement and 
recognition of the rights to use assets (including related to 
security deposits) and corresponding lease liabilities under 
IND AS 116.
Consolidated Performance
At a consolidated group level, the Company has achieved 
significant growth and profitability. The Consolidated Financial 
Highlights are given below:
Total Consolidated Revenue for FY 2024-25 is ` 17,134.61 
Crore as against ` 12,375.11 Crore in FY 2023-24, an increase 
by 38.46%. The EBITDA of Trent Group is ` 3,063.51 Crore 
(EBITDA - Profit Before Tax+ Finance Cost+Depreciation 
and Amortization Expense) for FY 2024-25, as against  
` 2,911.04 Crore in FY 2023-24, owing to higher revenues. 
Consolidated Profit After Tax is ` 1,534.41 Crore in  
FY 2024-25 as against ` 1,477.76 Crore in FY 2023-24 [which 
included an Exceptional Gain of ` 438.79 Crore, net of tax on 
account of reassessment of the estimates of measurement 
and recognition of the rights to use assets (including related 
to security deposits) and corresponding lease liabilities under 
IND AS 116].
The details of the performance of the Company’s Subsidiaries/ 
Joint Ventures (JVs)/Associates are covered below:
Subsidiaries, Joint Ventures and Associates
As on 31st March 2025, the Company has nine Subsidiary 
Companies (including two International Subsidiary Companies 
and one Section 8 Company), two Joint Ventures [one Joint 
Venture each with Tesco PLC, UK and MAS Amity Pte. Ltd. 
Singapore (‘MAS Amity’)] and two Associate Companies with 
Inditex, Spain.Dividend Distribution Policy
The Company has adopted a Dividend Distribution Policy 
in accordance with the Securities and Exchange Board of 
India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015 (‘the SEBI Listing Regulations’) with the 
objective of rewarding shareholders, retaining capital for 
growth and ensuring fairness and consistency in distributing 
profits to shareholders. The said Policy is available on the 
website of the Company at https://docs.trent-tata.com/
Dividend_Distribution_Policy.pdfDividend
The Board has recommended a Dividend of ` 5/- per share 
(500%) on 35,54,87,461 Equity Shares of ` 1/- each for the 
Financial Year ended 31st March 2025 [previous year: ` 3.20/- 
per Equity Share], subject to the approval of the shareholders 
at the ensuing Annual General Meeting (‘AGM’). The total 
Dividend on Equity Shares for FY 2024-25, if approved by 
the shareholders at the ensuing AGM, aggregate to ` 177.74 
Crore results in a Dividend pay-out of 11.22%.

Integrated Annual Report 2024-25The financial performance of the Company’s Subsidiaries, 
Joint Ventures and Associate companies for FY 2024-25 is 
provided below:
Subsidiary Companies
I. Booker India Limited (‘BIL’)
 BIL, a subsidiary of the Company, is engaged in 
wholesale cash and carry business, warehousing and 
other related services.
 Total Income of BIL is ` 167.01 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 179.96 Crore. 
Total Comprehensive Loss is ` 11.17 Crore as against 
its previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive Loss 
of ` 27.90 Crore.
II. Fiora Hypermarket Limited (‘FHL’)
 FHL, a wholly owned subsidiary of BIL, is engaged in 
the retail business under the Star banner and operation 
of Zudio stores.
 Total Income of FHL is ` 232.55 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 192.33 
Crore and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 3.28 
Crore as against its previous financial year’s Total 
Comprehensive Loss of ` 12.47 Crore.
III. Fiora Online Limited (‘FOL’)
 FOL, a wholly owned subsidiary of BIL, is engaged in 
online grocery retailing business under the brand name 
- StarQuik.
 Total Income of FOL is ` 114.86 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 133.97 Crore 
and Total Comprehensive Loss is ` 17.84 Crore as 
against its previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive 
Loss of ` 10.81 Crore.
 During the year, the Scheme of Amalgamation of FHL 
with FOL and its shareholders and creditors was filed 
with the National Company Law Tribunal, Mumbai, 
inter alia  with a view to enhance the operational, 
organizational and financial synergies between these 
companies. The Scheme is pending for approval by the 
requisite authorities.
IV. THPL Support Services Limited (‘TSSL’)
 TSSL is engaged in the business of warehousing and 
other related services. As a part of group restructuring 
and with an objective to reorganize and consolidate warehousing businesses and to enhance operational 
efficiencies, BIL acquired 100% shareholding of TSSL. 
Accordingly, TSSL is a wholly owned subsidiary of BIL 
w.e.f. 27th March 2025.
 Total Income of TSSL is ` 44.14 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 43.42 Crore 
and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 2.80 Crore as 
against its previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive 
Profit of ` 1.81 Crore.
V.  Fiora Business Support Services Limited (‘FBSSL’)
 FBSSL, a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, is 
engaged in the business of providing business support 
and outsourcing services relating to accounting, 
merchandising, human resources, payroll, etc.
 Total Income of FBSSL is ` 242.81 Crore as against 
its previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 168.94 
Crore and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 13.69 
Crore as against its previous financial year’s Total 
Comprehensive Income of ` 6.15 Crore.
VI. Nahar Retail Trading Services Limited (‘Nahar’)
 Nahar, a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company, 
is engaged in the business of franchising of fashion 
apparel retail stores of the Company besides managing 
a portfolio of real estate assets.
 Total Income of Nahar is ` 26.40 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 26.49 Crore 
and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 1.35 Crore as 
against its previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive 
Income of ` 3.08 Crore.
VII.  Trent Global Holdings Limited, Mauritius (‘TGHL’)
 TGHL, a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company in 
Mauritius, is engaged in the business of investment 
activities. Total Income of TGHL is ` 0.73 Crore and 
Total Comprehensive Loss is ` 0.60 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive Loss of  
` 0.24 Crore.
VIII. Trent Global Trading LLC, Dubai, UAE (‘TGTL’)
 TGTL was incorporated on 28th February 2024 as 
a wholly owned subsidiary of TGHL and runs retail 
stores of Zudio in Dubai. Total Income of TGTL is  
` 24.69 Crore and Total Comprehensive Loss is  
` 3.58 Crore.IX. Trent Foundation
 Trent Foundation was incorporated on 1st October 
2024, as a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company 
to undertake the Corporate Social Responsibility 
(CSR) activities focusing on areas viz. employability, 
education, entrepreneurship, environment, health and 
nutrition, water, etc and any other areas, as may be 
covered under Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 
2013 (‘the Act’).
Joint Venture companies
I. Trent Hypermarket Private Limited (‘THPL’)
 THPL, a Joint Venture of the Company, operates in the 
competitive food, grocery, and daily needs segment 
under the Star banner.
 Consolidated Total Income of THPL is ` 2,743.47 Crore 
as compared with its previous financial year’s Total 
Income of ` 2,210.53 Crore and Total Comprehensive 
Loss is ` 70.85 Crore as against its previous financial 
year’s Total Comprehensive Loss of ` 0.67 Crore. 
Total Comprehensive Income for FY 2024-25 includes 
Exceptional Gain of ` 18.53 Crore as compared with 
its previous financial year’s Exceptional Gain of ` 93.82 
Crore.
II. Trent MAS Fashion Private Limited (‘TMFPL’)
 TMFPL, a Joint Venture of the Company, is engaged 
in the business of design, product development,  
manufacture and import of all kinds of apparel and 
apparel related products including inner wear and 
lingerie wear.
 Total Income of TMFPL is ` 0.32 Crore as against its 
previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 0.03 Crore and 
Total Comprehensive Loss is ` 18.45 Crore as against 
its previous financial year’s Total Comprehensive Loss 
of ` 0.07 Crore.
Associate Companies
I.  Inditex Trent Retail India Private Limited (‘ITRIPL’)
 ITRIPL, an Associate of the Company, is engaged 
in operation of Zara stores in India. During the year, 
the Company offered 1,40,000 Equity Shares held 
by it in ITRIPL in the buyback offer made by ITRIPL. 
Consequent to the acceptance of the offer by ITRIPL, 
the Company holds 34.94% of the equity shareholding 
(earlier 49%) in ITRIPL w.e.f. 30th August 2024. Total Income of ITRIPL is ` 2,839.50 Crore as against 
its previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 2,776.67 
Crore and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 299.47 
Crore as against its previous financial year’s Total 
Comprehensive Income of ` 243.56 Crore.
II. Massimo Dutti India Private Limited (‘MDIPL’)
 MDIPL, an Associate of the Company, is engaged in 
operation of Massimo Dutti stores in India. During 
the year, pursuant to the offer received from Grupo 
Massimo Dutti, Spain (the JV Partner), the Company 
sold 1,75,450 Equity Shares (representing 29% of the 
equity shareholding) of MDIPL, to Grupo Massimo 
Dutti, Spain. Consequently, the Company holds 20% 
of the equity shareholding (earlier 49%) in MDIPL w.e.f 
25th March 2025.
 Total Income of MDIPL is ` 101.23 Crore as against 
its previous financial year’s Total Income of ` 101.79 
Crore and Total Comprehensive Income is ` 10.19 
Crore as against its previous financial year’s Total 
Comprehensive Income of ` 8.99 Crore.
The Company does not have a material subsidiary as on date 
as per the SEBI Listing Regulations.
A separate statement containing the salient features of the 
Financial Statements of the Company’s Subsidiaries, Joint 
Ventures and Associates, in Form AOC-1, forms part of 
the Consolidated Financial Statements, in compliance with 
Section 129(3) of the Act read with Rule 5 of Companies 
(Accounts) Rules, 2014, issued thereunder.
